- **random-forest-consensus-model-training**: Use when you have per-read methylation score files (ID/Chr/Pos/Strand/Score columns) from two or more nanopore CpG methylation tools run on the same reads, and need a consensus per-read methylation probability more accurate than either tool alone. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/random-forest-consensus-model-training))
- **random-forest-cross-validated-classification**: Use when you need a supervised classification baseline on peak-aligned metabolomics feature tables (e.g., cosine-similarity or spectral-entropy similarity tables) with labeled bioactivity groups, reported as accuracy, precision, recall, F1, and ROC AUC. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/random-forest-cross-validated-classification))
- **random-forest-cross-validated-hyperparameter-tuning**: Use when training a supervised loop-detection model from a 10 kb Hi-C cooler file and a bedpe positive set (e.g., H3K27ac HiChIP), needing per-chromosome models and tuned hyperparameters before scoring pixels. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/random-forest-cross-validated-hyperparameter-tuning))
- **random-forest-cross-validation**: Use when a variant prioritization pipeline needs an ex-ante pathogenicity probability per coding variant computed only from genomic and evolutionary annotations (allele frequency, conservation, deleteriousness predictors), before any phenotype or inheritance information is used. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/random-forest-cross-validation))
- **random-forest-cross-validation-classification**: Use when benchmarking how well cell-cell communication features (tensor factor loadings or UMAP embeddings from LR communication scores) separate biological phenotypes such as COVID-19 status and severity. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/random-forest-cross-validation-classification))
- **random-forest-cross-validation-evaluation**: Use when comparing feature subsets or classifiers for DNA 6mA sequence prediction on 41nt sequences and you need cross-validated AUC estimates (e.g. Binary vs Motif+Kmer+Binary) before freezing a feature matrix. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/random-forest-cross-validation-evaluation))
- **random-forest-discrimination-auc**: Decision support for quantifying how realistic synthetic scRNA-seq cells are: train a random forest on real-vs-generated labels and use the resulting AUC, where values near 0.5 (positive control ~0.52) indicate generated cells are indistinguishable from real PBMC cells. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/random-forest-discrimination-auc))
- **random-forest-dna-affinity-regression**: Use when predicting TF binding affinities from uPBM (35-mer oligos), gcPBM, or HT-SELEX datasets with a random forest over MD-derived DNA shape/flexibility features rather than sequence-only CNNs like DeepBind. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/random-forest-dna-affinity-regression))
- **random-forest-driver-gene-classification**: Use when classifying unlabelled cancer genes into TSGs/OGs from somatic mutation feature matrices (37 ratio-metric/entropy features), e.g. pan-cancer or tissue-specific driver gene prediction with CGC training labels. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/random-forest-driver-gene-classification))
- **random-forest-enhancer-classification**: Use when labeled enhancer and promoter (non-enhancer) genomic element sets are available with ChIP-seq and DNase-seq signal tracks (e.g. RPM-normalized bedGraph) and the goal is to train, validate, or apply a supervised enhancer-prediction model. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/random-forest-enhancer-classification))
- **random-forest-ensemble-inference**: Use when a small labelled gene set (e.g. 136 TSGs and 76 OGs from CGC) must be extended to unlabelled genes using somatic mutation-derived features, and consensus across ensemble fold models is needed to reduce overfitting. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/random-forest-ensemble-inference))
- **random-forest-ensemble-training**: Use when classifying candidate intronic terminal exons (or similar genomic regions) from conventional RNA-seq alignments, needing a supervised model with region-level features and held-out AUROC-style validation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/random-forest-ensemble-training))
- **random-forest-feature-importance-analysis**: Use when a trained random forest (e.g. scikit-learn) combines multiple candidate-scoring features and you must determine which feature contributes most to ranking performance. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/random-forest-feature-importance-analysis))
- **random-forest-feature-importance-extraction**: Use when comparing how training data source (uPBM vs gcPBM vs HT-SELEX) changes the relative importance of DNA feature classes in the DNAffinity random forest regressor, or when auditing which physical parameters drive affinity predictions. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/random-forest-feature-importance-extraction))
- **random-forest-feature-importance-extraction**: Use when trained random forest regression models (e.g. per-metabolite models over CCLE multiomics features) need their feature importances ranked, top-k features extracted, and robustness-confidence scores computed. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/random-forest-feature-importance-extraction))
- **random-forest-feature-importance-ranking**: Use when a small, class-imbalanced labelled gene set (e.g. 136 TSGs vs 76 OGs) must be ranked by which mutation-type features (entropy, frequency, ratio-metric) drive random forest classification, avoiding single-model importance instability. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/random-forest-feature-importance-ranking))
- **random-forest-gene-importance-ranking**: Use when a log-normalized scRNA-seq expression matrix with initial cluster labels needs a supervised, label-aware gene ranking (HIG) to complement unsupervised HVG selection before rare cell type detection. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/random-forest-gene-importance-ranking))
- **random-forest-gradient-boosting-classification**: Use when classifying single-cell RNA-seq cells from CKG or gene expression feature matrices with supervised tree-based models, or when RF/GBM feature_importances_ rankings are needed to compare feature representations. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/random-forest-gradient-boosting-classification))
- **random-forest-hyperparameter-tuning**: Use when classifying genes as TSGs vs OGs from a pre-processed mutation feature matrix (e.g. FeatureMat.tsv with 37 ratio-metric/entropy features) with CGC labels, and stable hyper-parameters are needed to avoid overfitting on a small labelled set. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/random-forest-hyperparameter-tuning))
- **random-forest-iterative-imputation**: Use when an n x p matrix with 10–30% of values removed completely at random contains mixed variable types (continuous + categorical) and a non-parametric imputation is needed, or when nonlinear relations/complex interactions are suspected. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/random-forest-iterative-imputation))
- **random-forest-learning-curve-analysis**: Use when you must show how a spectral classifier's precision and recall change as the training set is grown from small to full size, e.g. to justify a data-collection effort or reproduce a published learning curve. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/random-forest-learning-curve-analysis))
- **random-forest-lifestyle-classification**: Use when gene cluster absence/presence matrices paired with literature-based lifestyle metadata are available and the goal is to classify held-out genomes' lifestyle, including leave-one-species-out validation for novel species. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/random-forest-lifestyle-classification))
- **random-forest-matrix-completion**: Use when a preprocessed LC-MS MetaboSet object still contains NA values in exprs and a statistically sound fill-in (random forest, per Kokla et al.) is needed before feature-wise tests or multivariate models. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/random-forest-matrix-completion))
- **random-forest-metabolomics-classification**: Use when the analysis goal is supervised classification of metabolomics samples into class labels (e.g., treatment groups from a design file) and identification of which features differentiate those classes. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/random-forest-metabolomics-classification))
- **random-forest-missing-value-imputation**: Use when a behavioral item matrix (e.g. ASEBA CBCL parent-rated externalizing items) contains missing item responses and a completed matrix is required before factor extraction such as multiple correspondence analysis. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/random-forest-missing-value-imputation))
- **random-forest-model-evaluation**: Use when a metabolomics feature table with phenotype labels (e.g. CRC, polyp, healthy controls) needs supervised classification and validation of predictive performance via random forest OOB error rates. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/random-forest-model-evaluation))
- **random-forest-model-training**: Use when a two-class or multi-class phenotype classification model must be built from a merged MetaPhlAn table or QIIME/BIOM OTU table plus metadata (e.g. T1D t/f labels), and RF accuracy must be tuned and validated. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/random-forest-model-training))
- **random-forest-model-training-hic**: Use when a 10 kb-binned contact map (.cool or .hic) and a positive interaction list (bedpe from ChIA-PET, HiChIP, or annotated loops) are available and supervised chromatin loop detection is needed. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/random-forest-model-training-hic))
- **random-forest-model-training-on-3c-derived-interactions**: Use when you have a 10 kb-binned .cool or .hic Hi-C matrix and a bedpe list of positive interactions (ChIA-PET, HiChIP, Capture Hi-C) and need chromosome-wise random forest models for loop prediction. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/random-forest-model-training-on-3c-derived-interactions))
- **random-forest-model-training-per-chromosome**: Use when you have a .cool or .hic genome-wide contact map and an orthogonal positive interaction set (e.g. HiChIP/ChIA-PET bedpe) and need supervised loop classifiers for genome-wide prediction. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/random-forest-model-training-per-chromosome))
- **random-forest-normal-cancer-classification**: Use when you need computational validation that gene communities extracted from fused networks are relevant, by testing whether they classify TCGA normal/cancer samples better than DE or random gene sets. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/random-forest-normal-cancer-classification))
- **random-forest-oob-error-estimation**: Use when running missForest on data with artificially or naturally missing values and you need an imputation error estimate without holding out a test set, e.g. to report NRMSE/PFC reliability per simulation run. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/random-forest-oob-error-estimation))
- **random-forest-out-of-sample-classification-evaluation**: Use when you need a label-based, out-of-sample measure of embedding quality — e.g. scoring ivis embeddings of BMMC CyTOF subsamples with manually gated cell-population labels via held-out classification accuracy. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/random-forest-out-of-sample-classification-evaluation))
- **random-forest-peak-quality-classification**: Use when TARDIS-integrated targeted LC–MS peaks across biofluid matrices (stool, saliva, urine) have labeled quality classes and you need to reproduce or evaluate the RF 'Bad'-peak classifier and its variable-importance ranking. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/random-forest-peak-quality-classification))
- **random-forest-peak-quality-scoring**: Use when peaks have already been detected from .mzML HRMS scans with mssmain.peak_pick and you need to filter out poor-quality features (peak splitting, insufficient scan numbers, high baseline noise) using a pre-trained ML model. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/random-forest-peak-quality-scoring))
- **random-forest-permutation-feature-importance**: Use when a random-forest model (e.g., dsRID trained on Pacbio_AD_data.tsv) needs per-feature contribution scores rather than generic impurity-based importance, to explain which molecular traits drive dsRNA region prediction. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/random-forest-permutation-feature-importance))
- **random-forest-phenotype-classification**: Use when classifying metagenomic samples into phenotype classes (e.g., T2D, cirrhosis, T1D) from merged MetaPhlAn or QIIME/BIOM taxonomic profile tables with matching tab-separated metadata, and a robust RF-based accuracy estimate is needed. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/random-forest-phenotype-classification))
- **random-forest-pixel-classification**: Use when segmenting cells from multiplexed imaging data (e.g., multiplexed immunofluorescence colorectal adenoma images or MIBI breast carcinoma images) and you need pixel-level classification of cellular compartments to seed membrane-based resegmentation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/random-forest-pixel-classification))
- **random-forest-pixel-classification**: Use when multiplexed immunofluorescence or MIBI tissue images need pixel-level compartment labeling (nucleus, membrane, cytoplasm) to seed single-cell segmentation and enable per-cell marker quantification. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/random-forest-pixel-classification))
- **random-forest-probability-score-summarization**: Use when you have per-cell scATOMIC prediction scores from run_scATOMIC() across the pan-cancer TME hierarchy and need to convert raw class probabilities into per-layer confidence cut-offs and 'unclassified' relabelling. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/random-forest-probability-score-summarization))
- **random-forest-proximity-based-clustering**: Use when clustering high-dimensional tumor gene expression data (e.g., RNA-seq FPKM matrices) into stable, reproducible subgroups without predefining K, and when consensus clustering overfits due to the curse of dimensionality. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/random-forest-proximity-based-clustering))
- **random-forest-regression-model-fitting**: Use when a large-scale LC-MS metabolomics or proteomics feature table contains repeatedly injected pooled QC samples interleaved with biological samples (e.g. QC after every 5–8 injections) and per-feature intensities drift with injection order or vary across batches. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/random-forest-regression-model-fitting))
- **random-forest-regression-model-training**: Use when you have matched multi-omics and metabolite abundance matrices across samples (e.g. CCLE cancer cell lines) and need cross-validated, per-target regression to identify which feature set most accurately predicts each metabolite. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/random-forest-regression-model-training))
- **random-forest-regression-tuning**: Use when your MetaboSet object contains missing values (marked as NA) in the expression matrix after quality flagging, but you need complete data for multivariate analysis. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/random-forest-regression-tuning))
- **random-forest-regressor-training**: Use when k-mer binding data (e.g. HT-SELEX penultimate-cycle affinities, uPBM/gcPBM intensities) must be converted into per-protein affinity predictions via regression on MD-derived DNA shape features, with R2/Pearson/MSE evaluation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/random-forest-regressor-training))
- **random-forest-resource-prediction**: Use when you need to predict or characterize the computational cost (execution time, peak memory) of simulation methods across resampled dataset sizes from 100-10,000 cells and 500-10,000 genes. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics/tree/HEAD/collections/multi-omics/v1/leaves/random-forest-resource-prediction))
- **random-forest-retention-time-prediction**: Use when experimental MS1 features must be matched to MRN metabolites with a relative RT tolerance (30%) in addition to m/z, and only a subset of metabolites (seed annotations) have observed retention times to learn from. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/random-forest-retention-time-prediction))
- **random-forest-signaling-range-inference**: Use when you have scRNA-seq data with a spatial cell–cell distance matrix (e.g. from a SpaOTsc optimal transport mapping) and known ligand, receptor, and downstream gene lists, and you need to estimate the spatial range of a signaling pathway in micrometers. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/random-forest-signaling-range-inference))
- **random-forest-spectrum-pair-reranking**: Use when building or reproducing an MS2Query-style analogue search pipeline where MS2Deepscore preselection must be re-ranked to place the analogue or exact match at the top. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/random-forest-spectrum-pair-reranking))
- **random-forest-tree-number-tuning**: Use when building the RF component of an iPro-WAEL-style promoter classifier and you need to empirically select the number of trees via cross-validated grid search over promoter training sequences. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/random-forest-tree-number-tuning))
- **random-forest-variable-importance-extraction**: Use when a trained random forest model (e.g., eDiVA-Score's 1000-tree RF over 11 annotation features such as maximum MAF, PhastCons/PhyloP conservation, Condel, CADD, Eigen) must be interrogated to reproduce or report its feature-importance ranking. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/random-forest-variable-importance-extraction))
- **random-forest-variable-importance-ranking**: Use when a preprocessed, imputed LC-MS MetaboSet feature table with a class label (e.g. group column) is available and you need supervised, model-based identification of the most interesting molecular features rather than univariate feature-wise tests. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/random-forest-variable-importance-ranking))
- **random-forest-variant-classification**: Use when a raw genome-wide NanoSV VCF call set from MinION nanopore reads must be reduced to a high-confidence SV set, and a repeat filter has already (or will) produced the intermediate filtered call set the classifier is trained to score. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/random-forest-variant-classification))
- **random-forest-vs-logistic-regression-benchmarking**: Use when benchmarking which classifier (random forest vs logistic regression) better drives active learning cell selection from single-cell expression data when the initial training set is randomly chosen and may not contain all cell types. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/random-forest-vs-logistic-regression-benchmarking))
- **random-gene-set-size-sampling**: Use when benchmarking cell-type replicability (e.g., neuron vs non-neuron AUROC) with random gene sets and you need to test how set size (100 vs 800 genes) or cross-validation variability bins influence MetaNeighbor performance. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/random-gene-set-size-sampling))
- **random-generation**: 随机生成工具，提供随机数/字符串/字节/时长、数值范围、邮箱/手机/姓名/身份证/公司等假数据、域名关键词构建、姓氏管理与模型填充，当需要生成随机测试数据、随机ID片段、或构造仿真用户信息时使用 ([kamalyes/go-toolbox](https://github.com/kamalyes/go-toolbox/tree/HEAD/.vscode/skills/random-generation) · ★575)
- **random-geneset-null-distribution-sampling**: Use when quantifying pathway/module activity from a normalized gene expression matrix and you need empirical p-values for PC1-based activity statistics (L1, Median Exp) that distinguish shifted from overdispersed gene sets. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/random-geneset-null-distribution-sampling))
- **random-genome-subset-sampling**: Use when you need to validate whether a core-genome prediction quality estimate (e.g. mOTUpan --boots false-positive rate) reliably predicts true accuracy, especially for MAGs of varying completeness. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/random-genome-subset-sampling))
- **random-graph-datacenter-networks**: Random graph-based datacenter network design (RNG) for cost-optimized, fault-tolerant distributed systems. Covers distributed routing protocols exploiting random graph properties, passive optical cabling shuffles, and production deployment patterns. Use when: designing datacenter topologies, optimizing network cost vs performance, implementing distributed routing on non-hierarchical graphs, deploying fault-tolerant network fabrics, or evaluating random graph vs fat-tree tradeoffs. ([hiyenwong/ai_collection](https://github.com/hiyenwong/ai_collection/tree/HEAD/collection/skills/ai-ml/random-graph-datacenter-networks) · ★4 · 3 copies)
- **random-grover-search**: Randomized Grover search algorithm methodology that directly uses confidence-based sampling rather than amplitude amplification. ([hiyenwong/ai_collection](https://github.com/hiyenwong/ai_collection/tree/HEAD/collection/skills/quantum/random-grover-search) · ★4)
- **random-identifier-sampling**: Use when you need to quantify how reliably MetaFetcheR resolves identifiers for a given compound database — e.g. to reproduce the Supplementary Tables S2-S6 quality test before trusting mapping output. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/random-identifier-sampling))
- **random-injection**: Introduce genuine randomness into ideation by injecting an unrelated word or concept and forcing deep structural connections between it and the problem. Use when every other thinking approach has been exhausted, when output feels like it came from a brain that's been staring at the same problem too long, when structured creativity tools are producing diminishing returns, or when the user wants ideas from territory that no deliberate method can reach. - **random-walk-restart-gene-ranking**: Use when a curated seed gene set (e.g., MAPMAN- or GO-derived) must be scored or validated for functional interconnectivity on a multi-layer multiplex network, and left-out genes should be ranked by RWR proximity (AUROC/AUPRC). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/random-walk-restart-pathway-activity-scoring))

- **random-walk-trajectory-benchmarking**: Use when evaluating whether a lazy-teleporting random-walk/MCMC stage improves pseudotime accuracy on time-series single-cell (e.g., CyTOF) data against ablated variants and TI baselines. - **random-walk-transition-matrix-construction**: Use when you have a pre-processed N_cells x N_genes single-cell expression matrix and need to model cell-fate transition dynamics as a Markov random walk before attractor/coarse-graining analysis.
